kinetic energy is greater than the exothermic reaction

reaction thus forward reaction is favoured

temperature
as temperature increases, system re-establishes 
equilibrium by partially counteracting the increase in
temperature thus it favours the reaction which consumes
heat energy thus partially decreases temperature
thus the forward reaction is favoured as it is
endothermic and consumes heat energy. thus equilibrium
shfits right. thus to maximise yield of C2H4, temperature
needs to be increased.

as temperature increases, a larger proportion of reactants
and products have more kinetic energy
than activation energy. 
thus proportion of successful reactions increase
thus the rate of the forward
and reverse reaction increases. furthermore, increasing
temperature increases average velocity of all particles
in the system thus frequency of collisions of reactants
and products thus frequency of successful collisions
increase thus rate of forward and reverse reaction
increase.

however, a compromise must be made to reduce costs
and maximise safety as higher temperature requires
more expensive storage equipment and fuels to increase
temperature

pressure.

as pressure decreases, by le chatelier's principle, system
reestablishes equilibrium by partially increasing the
pressure, thus the forward reaction is favoured as it
produces more (2 moles) gas than the reverse (1 mole)
reaction. thus equilibrium shifts to the right and yield of
c2H4 increases thus lower pressure is prefered for higher
yield

as pressure increases, pressure of all gaseous species
in the system increases thus the frequency of collisions
thus successful collisions increase thus rate of
both forward and rate increase reaction. Thus to maximise
the rate of reaction a higher pressure is preferred.

a compromise must be made between high and low pressure
to optimise yield and rate of reaction.

pressure

partially counteracting the decrease in pressure thus it favours
the forward reaction as that produces more moles of gas than
the reverse reaction (at a ratio of 2:1 moles) thus equilibrium
shifts right and yield of c2h4 increases

and products increase thus frequency of successful collisions
increase thus rate of forward and reverse reaction increases.
thus to maximise rate, a higher pressure is preferred

thus a compromise must be made between high and low pressure
to maximise yield and rate.

			"updated": 1710571962847,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "adding water will dilute all solutions in the system\nthus concentration of all solution in the system decrease\nby le chateliers principle, system re-restablishes equilibrium\nby counteracting the decrease in concentration of\nall aqueous solution thus it favours the reverse\nreaction as that produces more moles of gas (5 moles)\nthan the forward reaction (1 mole)\n\nthus equilibrium shifts left and the solution turns from\nolive green into pale blue.",
			"rawText": "adding water will dilute all solutions in the system\nthus concentration of all solution in the system decrease\nby le chateliers principle, system re-restablishes equilibrium\nby counteracting the decrease in concentration of\nall aqueous solution thus it favours the reverse\nreaction as that produces more moles of gas (5 moles)\nthan the forward reaction (1 mole)\n\nthus equilibrium shifts left and the solution turns from\nolive green into pale blue.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "adding water will dilute all solutions in the system\nthus concentration of all solution in the system decrease\nby le chateliers principle, system re-restablishes equilibrium\nby counteracting the decrease in concentration of\nall aqueous solution thus it favours the reverse\nreaction as that produces more moles of gas (5 moles)\nthan the forward reaction (1 mole)\n\nthus equilibrium shifts left and the solution turns from\nolive green into pale blue.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 243
		},

		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 924,
			"versionNonce": 1006859848,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "mAuQ0ttt",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 2,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": 363.6532602976742,
			"y": -395.29528257387494,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 905.7592163085938,
			"height": 250,
			"seed": 855274897,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1710571962847,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "if temperature is doubled, average kinetic energy of all particles in the system\nwill increase thus more particles in the system will have more kinetic energy than activation\nenergy thus proporiton of successful collisions increase\nthus rate of both forward and reverse reaction increases.\nhowever as the forward reaction is endothermic and has a higher activation\nenergy than the reverse, a larger number of particles will have sufficient kinetic \nenergy to react thus the rate of endothermic reaction will increase more.\n\nas forward raction is favoured more products are produced than consumed.\nthus yield of methane increases thus [CH4(g)] increases",
			"rawText": "if temperature is doubled, average kinetic energy of all particles in the system\nwill increase thus more particles in the system will have more kinetic energy than activation\nenergy thus proporiton of successful collisions increase\nthus rate of both forward and reverse reaction increases.\nhowever as the forward reaction is endothermic and has a higher activation\nenergy than the reverse, a larger number of particles will have sufficient kinetic \nenergy to react thus the rate of endothermic reaction will increase more.\n\nas forward raction is favoured more products are produced than consumed.\nthus yield of methane increases thus [CH4(g)] increases",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "if temperature is doubled, average kinetic energy of all particles in the system\nwill increase thus more particles in the system will have more kinetic energy than activation\nenergy thus proporiton of successful collisions increase\nthus rate of both forward and reverse reaction increases.\nhowever as the forward reaction is endothermic and has a higher activation\nenergy than the reverse, a larger number of particles will have sufficient kinetic \nenergy to react thus the rate of endothermic reaction will increase more.\n\nas forward raction is favoured more products are produced than consumed.\nthus yield of methane increases thus [CH4(g)] increases",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 243
		},
		{
			"type": "text",

			"updated": 1710571962847,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "when you add water to the system, it decreases concentration of all\naqueous solution. thus system re establishes equilibrium by partially\ncounteracting the decrease inc oncentration thus it favours\nthe reaection which produces more mole of aqueous agents\nthus it favours the reverse recation as that produces more\nmoles (5 moles) of aqueous agents than the forward reaction\n(1 mole) thus equilibrium shifts left and yield of Cu(H2O)6 increases\nthus the solution becomes more blue. however, adding water discolourates\nthe system.",
			"rawText": "when you add water to the system, it decreases concentration of all\naqueous solution. thus system re establishes equilibrium by partially\ncounteracting the decrease inc oncentration thus it favours\nthe reaection which produces more mole of aqueous agents\nthus it favours the reverse recation as that produces more\nmoles (5 moles) of aqueous agents than the forward reaction\n(1 mole) thus equilibrium shifts left and yield of Cu(H2O)6 increases\nthus the solution becomes more blue. however, adding water discolourates\nthe system.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "when you add water to the system, it decreases concentration of all\naqueous solution. thus system re establishes equilibrium by partially\ncounteracting the decrease inc oncentration thus it favours\nthe reaection which produces more mole of aqueous agents\nthus it favours the reverse recation as that produces more\nmoles (5 moles) of aqueous agents than the forward reaction\n(1 mole) thus equilibrium shifts left and yield of Cu(H2O)6 increases\nthus the solution becomes more blue. however, adding water discolourates\nthe system.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 218
		},

			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "add IgA (aq)\n\nby adding IgA (aq), you increase concentration of [IgA] thus system\nre-establishes equilibrium by partially counteracting the increase in [IgA]\nthus it favours the forward reaction as that consumes IgA (aq)\nthus equilibrium shifts right and yield of IgB and Ih increase.\n\nThis decreases [Ck] thus decreasing immune system activity and increasing [Ih]\nfurther reducing immune system activity.",
			"rawText": "add IgA (aq)\n\nby adding IgA (aq), you increase concentration of [IgA] thus system\nre-establishes equilibrium by partially counteracting the increase in [IgA]\nthus it favours the forward reaction as that consumes IgA (aq)\nthus equilibrium shifts right and yield of IgB and Ih increase.\n\nThis decreases [Ck] thus decreasing immune system activity and increasing [Ih]\nfurther reducing immune system activity.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "add IgA (aq)\n\nby adding IgA (aq), you increase concentration of [IgA] thus system\nre-establishes equilibrium by partially counteracting the increase in [IgA]\nthus it favours the forward reaction as that consumes IgA (aq)\nthus equilibrium shifts right and yield of IgB and Ih increase.\n\nThis decreases [Ck] thus decreasing immune system activity and increasing [Ih]\nfurther reducing immune system activity.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,

		},

			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "no observable change would occur\n\nas a small amount is added, the increase in surface\narea is mostly negligible and as adding Ca(OH)2 does not affect\nQ as it is a solid, it does not affect equilibrium",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 118
		},
		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 719,
			"versionNonce": 749067592,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "xA04cwS0",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": 1446.8812053440358,
			"y": -45.8195697469437,
			"strokeColor": "#2f9e44",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 679.219482421875,
			"height": 275,
			"seed": 129809713,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1710571962848,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "the two lines going down can be observed as the reactants\nas the dotted line decreases twice as much as the full line\nas 2 moles of H2 (g) is consumed and 1 mole of CO(g) is consumed,\nthe dotted line is H2 (g) and full line is CO(g) and by process of \nelimination the dashed line is CH3OH\n\nat T1, the partial pressure of H2(g) suddenly increases, thus\nmore H2 is injected into the system at T1\n\nat T2, the partial pressure of CH3OH decreases, thus some CH3OH\nis removed from the system",
			"rawText": "the two lines going down can be observed as the reactants\nas the dotted line decreases twice as much as the full line\nas 2 moles of H2 (g) is consumed and 1 mole of CO(g) is consumed,\nthe dotted line is H2 (g) and full line is CO(g) and by process of \nelimination the dashed line is CH3OH\n\nat T1, the partial pressure of H2(g) suddenly increases, thus\nmore H2 is injected into the system at T1\n\nat T2, the partial pressure of CH3OH decreases, thus some CH3OH\nis removed from the system",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "the two lines going down can be observed as the reactants\nas the dotted line decreases twice as much as the full line\nas 2 moles of H2 (g) is consumed and 1 mole of CO(g) is consumed,\nthe dotted line is H2 (g) and full line is CO(g) and by process of \nelimination the dashed line is CH3OH\n\nat T1, the partial pressure of H2(g) suddenly increases, thus\nmore H2 is injected into the system at T1\n\nat T2, the partial pressure of CH3OH decreases, thus some CH3OH\nis removed from the system",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 268
		},
		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 949,
			"versionNonce": 1059552056,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "aywJdR9K",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": 1499.719183148025,
			"y": 317.47374593457795,
			"strokeColor": "#2f9e44",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 642.8595581054688,
			"height": 350,
			"seed": 1346679807,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1710571962848,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "if test tube temperature is decreased, average kinetic\nenergy of all particles in the system decreases, thus \na lower number of particles have more kinetic energy\nthan activation energy. thus both rate of forward\nand reverse reaction decreases. as the endothermic reaction\nhas a larger activation energy than the exothermic reaction\na larger proportion of particles will not have sufficient kinetic\nenergy thus the rate of reaction would decrease relatively more\nthan the exothermic reaction.\nas the forward reaction is endothermic, the rate decreases\nmore than the reverse, thus more reactants are produced than \nconsumed as equilibrium shifts right.\n\nthus yield of Ca(OH)2 increases and Ca(OH)2 increases in size",
			"rawText": "if test tube temperature is decreased, average kinetic\nenergy of all particles in the system decreases, thus \na lower number of particles have more kinetic energy\nthan activation energy. thus both rate of forward\nand reverse reaction decreases. as the endothermic reaction\nhas a larger activation energy than the exothermic reaction\na larger proportion of particles will not have sufficient kinetic\nenergy thus the rate of reaction would decrease relatively more\nthan the exothermic reaction.\nas the forward reaction is endothermic, the rate decreases\nmore than the reverse, thus more reactants are produced than \nconsumed as equilibrium shifts right.\n\nthus yield of Ca(OH)2 increases and Ca(OH)2 increases in size",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "if test tube temperature is decreased, average kinetic\nenergy of all particles in the system decreases, thus \na lower number of particles have more kinetic energy\nthan activation energy. thus both rate of forward\nand reverse reaction decreases. as the endothermic reaction\nhas a larger activation energy than the exothermic reaction\na larger proportion of particles will not have sufficient kinetic\nenergy thus the rate of reaction would decrease relatively more\nthan the exothermic reaction.\nas the forward reaction is endothermic, the rate decreases\nmore than the reverse, thus more reactants are produced than \nconsumed as equilibrium shifts right.\n\nthus yield of Ca(OH)2 increases and Ca(OH)2 increases in size",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 343
		},

			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "solid precipitate gains mass and increases in size.\n\nif temperature decreases by le chatelier's principle, system restablishes\nequilibrium by partially counteracting the decrease in temperature. \nthus it favours the reverse reaction as that produces heat energy\nthus partially increase the temperature of the system.\nthus equilibrium shifts to the left and yield of Ca(OH)2 increases",

			"updated": 1710571962849,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "when [OH-] increases, pH increases and thus [H+] decreases\nas [H+] decreases, frequency of collisions \nthus successful collisions of the reverse\nreaction in the second reaction decrease thus \nrate of the reverse reaction decrease.\n\nas the rate of the forward reaction initially remains unchanged\nthe rate of the reverse reaction is more than the rate of the\nforward reaction thus more products are formed than reactants.\n\nthus more CO2(aq) is consumed thus more CO2(aq) thus\n[CO2(aq)] decreases thus [CO2(g)] decrease",
			"rawText": "when [OH-] increases, pH increases and thus [H+] decreases\nas [H+] decreases, frequency of collisions \nthus successful collisions of the reverse\nreaction in the second reaction decrease thus \nrate of the reverse reaction decrease.\n\nas the rate of the forward reaction initially remains unchanged\nthe rate of the reverse reaction is more than the rate of the\nforward reaction thus more products are formed than reactants.\n\nthus more CO2(aq) is consumed thus more CO2(aq) thus\n[CO2(aq)] decreases thus [CO2(g)] decrease",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "when [OH-] increases, pH increases and thus [H+] decreases\nas [H+] decreases, frequency of collisions \nthus successful collisions of the reverse\nreaction in the second reaction decrease thus \nrate of the reverse reaction decrease.\n\nas the rate of the forward reaction initially remains unchanged\nthe rate of the reverse reaction is more than the rate of the\nforward reaction thus more products are formed than reactants.\n\nthus more CO2(aq) is consumed thus more CO2(aq) thus\n[CO2(aq)] decreases thus [CO2(g)] decrease",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 293
		},

			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "decrease water increases concentration of all\naqueous species in the system.\n\nthus frequency of collisions of forward and reverse\nreaciton increase thus successful collisions increase\nthus rate of forward and reverse reaction increase.\nas the reverse reaction consumes two moles of aqueous\nspecies compared to the one mole of forward reaction.\nthus the increase in frequency of collisions thus successful\ncollisions increase greater.\n\nthus more reactants are produced than consumed \nthus yield of CO2(aq) and [CO2(aq)] increase\nthus more CO2(g) is produced",
			"rawText": "decrease water increases concentration of all\naqueous species in the system.\n\nthus frequency of collisions of forward and reverse\nreaciton increase thus successful collisions increase\nthus rate of forward and reverse reaction increase.\nas the reverse reaction consumes two moles of aqueous\nspecies compared to the one mole of forward reaction.\nthus the increase in frequency of collisions thus successful\ncollisions increase greater.\n\nthus more reactants are produced than consumed \nthus yield of CO2(aq) and [CO2(aq)] increase\nthus more CO2(g) is produced",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "decrease water increases concentration of all\naqueous species in the system.\n\nthus frequency of collisions of forward and reverse\nreaciton increase thus successful collisions increase\nthus rate of forward and reverse reaction increase.\nas the reverse reaction consumes two moles of aqueous\nspecies compared to the one mole of forward reaction.\nthus the increase in frequency of collisions thus successful\ncollisions increase greater.\n\nthus more reactants are produced than consumed \nthus yield of CO2(aq) and [CO2(aq)] increase\nthus more CO2(g) is produced",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,

		{
			"type": "text",

			"updated": 1710571962849,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "in human acidosis, pH drops below 6 thus [H+] increases\nin the 2nd reaction, the frequency of collisions of thus\nfrequency of successful collisions in the \nreverse reaction increases. as initially the forward\nreaction is unchanged, rate of reaction\nof the forward reaction is lower than the rate of\nthe reverse reaction. thus ",
			"rawText": "in human acidosis, pH drops below 6 thus [H+] increases\nin the 2nd reaction, the frequency of collisions of thus\nfrequency of successful collisions in the \nreverse reaction increases. as initially the forward\nreaction is unchanged, rate of reaction\nof the forward reaction is lower than the rate of\nthe reverse reaction. thus ",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "in human acidosis, pH drops below 6 thus [H+] increases\nin the 2nd reaction, the frequency of collisions of thus\nfrequency of successful collisions in the \nreverse reaction increases. as initially the forward\nreaction is unchanged, rate of reaction\nof the forward reaction is lower than the rate of\nthe reverse reaction. thus ",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 168
		},
		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 825,
			"versionNonce": 404003640,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "RQgZSj1L",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "dashed",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": 3121.963046021907,
			"y": 2058.082762484944,
			"strokeColor": "#1971c2",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 640.4395751953125,
			"height": 325,
			"seed": 251694047,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1710571962849,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "as pH is extremely low, as pH = -log[H+], \n[H+] is extremely high. thus, the frequency of collisions\nthus frequency of successful collisions between products\nin reaction 2 increases. as initially, the rate of the\nforward reaction remains unchanged, the rate\nof the reverse reaction is higher than the rate of the\nforward reaction, thus more reactants are produced than\nconsumed as rate of reverse reaction gradually\ndecreases and rate of forward reaction gradually\nincreases.\n\nthis continues until the two arates are equal and at net higher\nreaction rate eqm shift left",
			"rawText": "as pH is extremely low, as pH = -log[H+], \n[H+] is extremely high. thus, the frequency of collisions\nthus frequency of successful collisions between products\nin reaction 2 increases. as initially, the rate of the\nforward reaction remains unchanged, the rate\nof the reverse reaction is higher than the rate of the\nforward reaction, thus more reactants are produced than\nconsumed as rate of reverse reaction gradually\ndecreases and rate of forward reaction gradually\nincreases.\n\nthis continues until the two arates are equal and at net higher\nreaction rate eqm shift left",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "as pH is extremely low, as pH = -log[H+], \n[H+] is extremely high. thus, the frequency of collisions\nthus frequency of successful collisions between products\nin reaction 2 increases. as initially, the rate of the\nforward reaction remains unchanged, the rate\nof the reverse reaction is higher than the rate of the\nforward reaction, thus more reactants are produced than\nconsumed as rate of reverse reaction gradually\ndecreases and rate of forward reaction gradually\nincreases.\n\nthis continues until the two arates are equal and at net higher\nreaction rate eqm shift left",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 318
		},

		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 2847,
			"versionNonce": 235801416,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "FXEHmWaj",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "dashed",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": -291.2157908820391,
			"y": 1893.832793452148,
			"strokeColor": "#1971c2",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 593.6195068359375,
			"height": 1325,
			"seed": 139436945,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1710571962849,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "temperature\nas temperature increases, system re-establishes \nequilibrium by partially counteracting the increase in\ntemperature thus it favours the reaction which consumes\nheat energy thus partially decreases temperature\nthus the forward reaction is favoured as it is\nendothermic and consumes heat energy. thus equilibrium\nshfits right. thus to maximise yield of C2H4, temperature\nneeds to be increased.\n\nas temperature increases, a larger proportion of reactants\nand products have more kinetic energy\nthan activation energy. \nthus proportion of successful reactions increase\nthus the rate of the forward\nand reverse reaction increases. furthermore, increasing\ntemperature increases average velocity of all particles\nin the system thus frequency of collisions of reactants\nand products thus frequency of successful collisions\nincrease thus rate of forward and reverse reaction\nincrease.\n\nhowever, a compromise must be made to reduce costs\nand maximise safety as higher temperature requires\nmore expensive storage equipment and fuels to increase\ntemperature\n\npressure.\n\nas pressure decreases, by le chatelier's principle, system\nreestablishes equilibrium by partially increasing the\npressure, thus the forward reaction is favoured as it\nproduces more (2 moles) gas than the reverse (1 mole)\nreaction. thus equilibrium shifts to the right and yield of\nc2H4 increases thus lower pressure is prefered for higher\nyield\n\nas pressure increases, pressure of all gaseous species\nin the system increases thus the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions increase thus rate of\nboth forward and rate increase reaction. Thus to maximise\nthe rate of reaction a higher pressure is preferred.\n\na compromise must be made between high and low pressure\nto optimise yield and rate of reaction.\n\neconomics:\n\nsulphuric acid increases rate of forward and reverse\nreaction without being consumed by lowering the activation\nenergy by providing an alternate pathway to the reaction.\nthus it saves cost as a lower pressure and temperature\ncan be used ",
			"rawText": "temperature\nas temperature increases, system re-establishes \nequilibrium by partially counteracting the increase in\ntemperature thus it favours the reaction which consumes\nheat energy thus partially decreases temperature\nthus the forward reaction is favoured as it is\nendothermic and consumes heat energy. thus equilibrium\nshfits right. thus to maximise yield of C2H4, temperature\nneeds to be increased.\n\nas temperature increases, a larger proportion of reactants\nand products have more kinetic energy\nthan activation energy. \nthus proportion of successful reactions increase\nthus the rate of the forward\nand reverse reaction increases. furthermore, increasing\ntemperature increases average velocity of all particles\nin the system thus frequency of collisions of reactants\nand products thus frequency of successful collisions\nincrease thus rate of forward and reverse reaction\nincrease.\n\nhowever, a compromise must be made to reduce costs\nand maximise safety as higher temperature requires\nmore expensive storage equipment and fuels to increase\ntemperature\n\npressure.\n\nas pressure decreases, by le chatelier's principle, system\nreestablishes equilibrium by partially increasing the\npressure, thus the forward reaction is favoured as it\nproduces more (2 moles) gas than the reverse (1 mole)\nreaction. thus equilibrium shifts to the right and yield of\nc2H4 increases thus lower pressure is prefered for higher\nyield\n\nas pressure increases, pressure of all gaseous species\nin the system increases thus the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions increase thus rate of\nboth forward and rate increase reaction. Thus to maximise\nthe rate of reaction a higher pressure is preferred.\n\na compromise must be made between high and low pressure\nto optimise yield and rate of reaction.\n\neconomics:\n\nsulphuric acid increases rate of forward and reverse\nreaction without being consumed by lowering the activation\nenergy by providing an alternate pathway to the reaction.\nthus it saves cost as a lower pressure and temperature\ncan be used ",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "temperature\nas temperature increases, system re-establishes \nequilibrium by partially counteracting the increase in\ntemperature thus it favours the reaction which consumes\nheat energy thus partially decreases temperature\nthus the forward reaction is favoured as it is\nendothermic and consumes heat energy. thus equilibrium\nshfits right. thus to maximise yield of C2H4, temperature\nneeds to be increased.\n\nas temperature increases, a larger proportion of reactants\nand products have more kinetic energy\nthan activation energy. \nthus proportion of successful reactions increase\nthus the rate of the forward\nand reverse reaction increases. furthermore, increasing\ntemperature increases average velocity of all particles\nin the system thus frequency of collisions of reactants\nand products thus frequency of successful collisions\nincrease thus rate of forward and reverse reaction\nincrease.\n\nhowever, a compromise must be made to reduce costs\nand maximise safety as higher temperature requires\nmore expensive storage equipment and fuels to increase\ntemperature\n\npressure.\n\nas pressure decreases, by le chatelier's principle, system\nreestablishes equilibrium by partially increasing the\npressure, thus the forward reaction is favoured as it\nproduces more (2 moles) gas than the reverse (1 mole)\nreaction. thus equilibrium shifts to the right and yield of\nc2H4 increases thus lower pressure is prefered for higher\nyield\n\nas pressure increases, pressure of all gaseous species\nin the system increases thus the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions increase thus rate of\nboth forward and rate increase reaction. Thus to maximise\nthe rate of reaction a higher pressure is preferred.\n\na compromise must be made between high and low pressure\nto optimise yield and rate of reaction.\n\neconomics:\n\nsulphuric acid increases rate of forward and reverse\nreaction without being consumed by lowering the activation\nenergy by providing an alternate pathway to the reaction.\nthus it saves cost as a lower pressure and temperature\ncan be used ",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,

			"width": 587.99951171875,

			"groupIds": [],

			"updated": 1710571962849,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "as pH is extremely low, and pH = -log[H+], concentration\nof [H+] is extremely high. Thus the frequency of collisions\nbetween products in the second reaction increased\nthus frequency of successful collisions increase, thus\nrate of reverse reaction increase.\n\nas the rate of forward reaction remains initially unchanged\nthe rate of reverse reaction is greater than the rate\nof the forward reaction thus more reactants are being\nproduced than consumed.\n\nrate of reverse reaction gradually decrease and\nrate of forward reaction gradually increases \nuntil the two rates are constant and equal at a higher\nrate than before acidosis.\n\nvolume of water has increased, and thus the body\nresponds to this by urinating more to get rid of excess\nwater and urine that is more concentrated in H+ ions",
			"rawText": "as pH is extremely low, and pH = -log[H+], concentration\nof [H+] is extremely high. Thus the frequency of collisions\nbetween products in the second reaction increased\nthus frequency of successful collisions increase, thus\nrate of reverse reaction increase.\n\nas the rate of forward reaction remains initially unchanged\nthe rate of reverse reaction is greater than the rate\nof the forward reaction thus more reactants are being\nproduced than consumed.\n\nrate of reverse reaction gradually decrease and\nrate of forward reaction gradually increases \nuntil the two rates are constant and equal at a higher\nrate than before acidosis.\n\nvolume of water has increased, and thus the body\nresponds to this by urinating more to get rid of excess\nwater and urine that is more concentrated in H+ ions",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "as pH is extremely low, and pH = -log[H+], concentration\nof [H+] is extremely high. Thus the frequency of collisions\nbetween products in the second reaction increased\nthus frequency of successful collisions increase, thus\nrate of reverse reaction increase.\n\nas the rate of forward reaction remains initially unchanged\nthe rate of reverse reaction is greater than the rate\nof the forward reaction thus more reactants are being\nproduced than consumed.\n\nrate of reverse reaction gradually decrease and\nrate of forward reaction gradually increases \nuntil the two rates are constant and equal at a higher\nrate than before acidosis.\n\nvolume of water has increased, and thus the body\nresponds to this by urinating more to get rid of excess\nwater and urine that is more concentrated in H+ ions",

		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 2344,
			"versionNonce": 1533663560,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "doThK9ub",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "dashed",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": 387.3326519866988,
			"y": 3378.926543525594,
			"strokeColor": "#f08c00",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 657.659423828125,
			"height": 1000,
			"seed": 1930548593,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1710571962850,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "temperature\nas temperature increases, system re-establishes equilibrium\nby partially counteracting the increase in temperature\nthus it favours the forward reaction as it is endothermic\nand thus partially decreases temperature. thus equilibrium shifts\nto right and yield of c2h4 (g) increases.\n\nas temperature increases, average kientic energy increases\nthus higher proportion of products and\nreactants  have greater kinetic\nenergy than activation energy thus rate of both forward\nand reverse reaction increases.\n\nthus to maximise yield and rate of reaciton, a higher tempearture\nis preferred, however, a compromise must bbe made as higher\ntemperature increases safety risks and operating cost\nto maintain the higher temperature\n\npressure\nas pressure decreases, system re-establishes equilibrium by\npartially counteracting the decrease in pressure thus it favours\nthe forward reaction as that produces more moles of gas than\nthe reverse reaction (at a ratio of 2:1 moles) thus equilibrium\nshifts right and yield of c2h4 increases\nthus to increase yield, pressure needs to be decreased\n\nas pressure increases, frequency of collisions between reactants\nand products increase thus frequency of successful collisions\nincrease thus rate of forward and reverse reaction increases.\nthus to maximise rate, a higher pressure is preferred\n\nthus a compromise must be made between high and low pressure\nto maximise yield and rate.\n\ncataylst\na sulphuric acid catalyst is used as it provides an alternate\npathway to the reaction decreasing activation energy without\nbeing consumed. the presence of sulphuric acid catalyst increases\nrate without sacrificing yield, thus temperature and pressure\ncan be lowered improving costs.",
			"rawText": "temperature\nas temperature increases, system re-establishes equilibrium\nby partially counteracting the increase in temperature\nthus it favours the forward reaction as it is endothermic\nand thus partially decreases temperature. thus equilibrium shifts\nto right and yield of c2h4 (g) increases.\n\nas temperature increases, average kientic energy increases\nthus higher proportion of products and\nreactants  have greater kinetic\nenergy than activation energy thus rate of both forward\nand reverse reaction increases.\n\nthus to maximise yield and rate of reaciton, a higher tempearture\nis preferred, however, a compromise must bbe made as higher\ntemperature increases safety risks and operating cost\nto maintain the higher temperature\n\npressure\nas pressure decreases, system re-establishes equilibrium by\npartially counteracting the decrease in pressure thus it favours\nthe forward reaction as that produces more moles of gas than\nthe reverse reaction (at a ratio of 2:1 moles) thus equilibrium\nshifts right and yield of c2h4 increases\nthus to increase yield, pressure needs to be decreased\n\nas pressure increases, frequency of collisions between reactants\nand products increase thus frequency of successful collisions\nincrease thus rate of forward and reverse reaction increases.\nthus to maximise rate, a higher pressure is preferred\n\nthus a compromise must be made between high and low pressure\nto maximise yield and rate.\n\ncataylst\na sulphuric acid catalyst is used as it provides an alternate\npathway to the reaction decreasing activation energy without\nbeing consumed. the presence of sulphuric acid catalyst increases\nrate without sacrificing yield, thus temperature and pressure\ncan be lowered improving costs.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "temperature\nas temperature increases, system re-establishes equilibrium\nby partially counteracting the increase in temperature\nthus it favours the forward reaction as it is endothermic\nand thus partially decreases temperature. thus equilibrium shifts\nto right and yield of c2h4 (g) increases.\n\nas temperature increases, average kientic energy increases\nthus higher proportion of products and\nreactants  have greater kinetic\nenergy than activation energy thus rate of both forward\nand reverse reaction increases.\n\nthus to maximise yield and rate of reaciton, a higher tempearture\nis preferred, however, a compromise must bbe made as higher\ntemperature increases safety risks and operating cost\nto maintain the higher temperature\n\npressure\nas pressure decreases, system re-establishes equilibrium by\npartially counteracting the decrease in pressure thus it favours\nthe forward reaction as that produces more moles of gas than\nthe reverse reaction (at a ratio of 2:1 moles) thus equilibrium\nshifts right and yield of c2h4 increases\nthus to increase yield, pressure needs to be decreased\n\nas pressure increases, frequency of collisions between reactants\nand products increase thus frequency of successful collisions\nincrease thus rate of forward and reverse reaction increases.\nthus to maximise rate, a higher pressure is preferred\n\nthus a compromise must be made between high and low pressure\nto maximise yield and rate.\n\ncataylst\na sulphuric acid catalyst is used as it provides an alternate\npathway to the reaction decreasing activation energy without\nbeing consumed. the presence of sulphuric acid catalyst increases\nrate without sacrificing yield, thus temperature and pressure\ncan be lowered improving costs.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 993
		},

		{

			"angle": 0,
			"x": 1687.7006621205476,
			"y": 3316.7646619943293,

			"width": 705.599365234375,

			"updated": 1710571962850,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "adding hcl to the solution increases concentration of cl- thus\nincreasing frequency of collisons thus frequency of successful collisions\nbetween products thus rate of reverse reaction increases.\n\nas initially the forward reaction remains unchanged, the rate of \nthe reverse reaction is greater than the forward reaction thus more\nreactants are being produced than consumed.\n\nthus equilibrium shifts left and rate of forward reaction gradually\nincreases whilst rate of reverse reaction gradually decreases until\nthe rate of the forward and reverse reaction is equal and constant\nat a higher equilibrium before addition of HCl. concentratio of products\nand reactants are increased thus pH is lower.",
			"rawText": "adding hcl to the solution increases concentration of cl- thus\nincreasing frequency of collisons thus frequency of successful collisions\nbetween products thus rate of reverse reaction increases.\n\nas initially the forward reaction remains unchanged, the rate of \nthe reverse reaction is greater than the forward reaction thus more\nreactants are being produced than consumed.\n\nthus equilibrium shifts left and rate of forward reaction gradually\nincreases whilst rate of reverse reaction gradually decreases until\nthe rate of the forward and reverse reaction is equal and constant\nat a higher equilibrium before addition of HCl. concentratio of products\nand reactants are increased thus pH is lower.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "adding hcl to the solution increases concentration of cl- thus\nincreasing frequency of collisons thus frequency of successful collisions\nbetween products thus rate of reverse reaction increases.\n\nas initially the forward reaction remains unchanged, the rate of \nthe reverse reaction is greater than the forward reaction thus more\nreactants are being produced than consumed.\n\nthus equilibrium shifts left and rate of forward reaction gradually\nincreases whilst rate of reverse reaction gradually decreases until\nthe rate of the forward and reverse reaction is equal and constant\nat a higher equilibrium before addition of HCl. concentratio of products\nand reactants are increased thus pH is lower.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 318
		},
